Let's think of specific constructions that flatten convolution. The problem of minimizing maximum of f*f for nonnegative sum 1 is equivalent to minimizing the l∞ norm of the "sum distribution" of a random variable taking values 0..n-1 with probabilities p_i=f_i/S. We want the distribution of X+Y (X,Y iid with distribution p) to be as flat as possible over 0..2n-2. This is exactly designing a probability distribution on a finite interval whose self-convolution (distribution of sum) has minimal peak. The baseline uniform on interval gives triangular peak n. Two-level half-half gives sum distribution plateau with peak 4/9 n? Wait peak probability P(Sum=k) = c_k/S^2. For two-level normalized [2/3,1/3], peak= (4m)/(9m^2)=4/(9m)=2/(9/2 n)? For n=2m, peak/S^2? E=2n peak probability? E=2n * max c/S^2 = 2n * peak_prob. So two-level peak_prob = E/(2n)=8/(9n). Average peak over 2n-1 =1/(2n-1) ~1/(2n). Ratio to average = (8/9n)/(1/(2n))=16/9 ≈1.777. We want peak_prob = E/(2n)=0.75135/n, average 1/(2n), ratio to average=1.5027. So need sum distribution peak only 1.5 times average, quite flat.

This is achievable? For finite interval, by Sklar? If we choose p with support such that X+Y support has many points and distribution flat. But support length n gives sum support 2n-1. If p is uniform over a Sidon-like set? Let p be uniform over k points out of n. Then sum support size <= k^2, but max count at most? For random X uniform on set A, P(X+Y=s) = |{a+b=s}|/k^2. Maximum could be small if A is a Sidon set (all sums distinct) then max=1/k^2, but sum values up to 2n? Average over 2n-1 is 1/(2n-1), but over support k^2 maybe. The objective uses max over all 2n-1 positions; if support has holes, holes zero but max over support. We want max count/k^2. If A is Sidon, max=1/k^2. Then E = 2n * (1/k^2) * S^2? Wait p uniform on k points: f_i=1/k on A, S=1. c_max = max count / k^2. E=2n * max_count/k^2. If A is Sidon, max_count <=1 (for s not maybe double? Actually equation a+b=s can have multiple ordered pairs even if set of sums unique; max ordered pairs maybe 1 or 2). Then E ~ 2n/k^2. But Sidon sets in [0,n-1] have size k ~ sqrt(n). What about using a sequence that is a probability distribution with maximum sum distribution small. This is related to "additive combinatorics": minimize max |A+A| counts weighted. For p uniform on a set A of size k in group Z (not cyclic), max r_{A+A}(s). We need n large. The objective E=2n * max_s r(s)/k^2. We want max_s r(s) as small as possible relative to n/k^2. We need practical algorithm. Let's design a robust optimizer using first-order methods in log-linear variables, with multiple restarts/lengths, and maybe exploit fast convolution. We need ensure final below target. We can also incorporate known constructive patterns: linear ramp? half-block? Maybe a "tent" non-palindromic? Let's test some parametric families that might yield low E, and use them as initializations. We can include a small grid search over shapes and n, then gradient refine.

Parametric families:
1. Two-level half-block: E=16/9 ~1.777.
2. Exponential/geometric decaying? f_i ∝ r^i? Let's analyze. For geometric f_i = ρ^i, sum S=(1-ρ^n)/(1-ρ). Convolution c_k = ρ^k (1-ρ^{min(k+1,n,k-n+2)})/(1-ρ)^2. Max likely near center k=n-1: c≈ρ^{n-1}(1-ρ^n)/(1-ρ)^2? Actually for k=n-1, pairs i=0..n-1: ρ^{2i+k?} wait f_i f_{n-1-i}=ρ^{n-1}. Sum n ρ^{n-1}. Edges c_0=1. If ρ<1, max might be c_0=1 vs center nρ^{n-1}. Choose ρ to balance: nρ^{n-1}=1 => ρ≈n^{-1/(n-1)}≈e^{-ln n/(n-1)} ≈1 - ln n/n. Sum ≈ n(1 - ln n/n)^n ≈ n e^{-ln n}=1. So S≈1, max≈1, E≈2n huge? Wait if max absolute 1 and S≈1, E=2n, terrible. Scale invariant: if f not normalized, cmax~1, S~1? E large. If scale to S=1, cmax~1, E~2n. Bad. Geometric terrible because long length amplifies.

3. Parabolic/bell shape: f_i = 1 - (i - center)^2? Non-palindrome? If centered, palindrome, center peak high. If skewed maybe.

4. Sequence based on derivative of uniform? Need flatten sum distribution. Maybe choose p with compact support and density proportional to a window whose convolution flat. We can solve via iterative flattening.

Let's formulate optimization more concretely and think about convergence.

Normalize p_i >=0, sum=1. Objective J= max_k (p*p)_k. E=2n J.
We can use soft-max with temperature α on c = p*p. Since sum=1, c values ~1/n. α large ~n maybe for precision. Gradient via FFT.

Parameterization: p_i = softmax(z_i) (sum1). Use Adam on z (length n). Initial z from shapes.

However softmax(0)=uniform gives J~1/n, Adam may slowly move. Need good initial and step sizes.

Alternative: direct p with multiplicative weights:
At each iteration:
- c = convolve(p,p) (FFT)
- weights w = softmax(α(c - max?)) maybe normalized length L=2n-1. If use top-k weights, maybe easier.
- g_i = corr(p, w) (as above) = Σ_j p_j w_{i+j}.
- Update p_i ← p_i * exp(-η * g_i / p_i? or g_i?) then normalize.

Because p sum fixed, if we update p_i by multiplicative factors and renormalize, invariant. What factor? We want reduce active peaks. If p_i increases, peaks where i participates increase. The derivative of c_k wrt log p_i (with sum not fixed) is 2 p_i f_{k-i}. If we parameterize log p up to additive constant (so sum fixed after normalization), natural update log p_i -= η * Σ w_k 2 p_i p_{k-i}. That is factor exp(-η * 2 p_i L_i). For uniform p=1/n, L_i ~ active/n, so exponent ~ -η *2/n^2 small; choose η ~ n maybe. But for nonuniform, could be large. We can scale by mean.

Update: p_i *= exp(-lr * (p_i * L_i) / max_i(p_i L_i))? Then normalize. This is like gradient in log domain. Need choose lr ~0.5? Maybe.

But if p_i small, p_i L_i small, relative change small; small components may persist. If p_i zero, stay zero. We may want all positive. Start positive.

Alternatively, update z_i (log p before normalization) -= lr * (p_i L_i) * n? Then p=softmax(z). This ensures positivity.

Let's design a robust first-order optimizer:
- Use z variables, p=softmax(z). To avoid underflow, store z centered (z -= mean(z)). p = exp(z - logsumexp).
- Each iteration: c = fft_conv(p,p). L=2n-1.
- Compute weights w = softmax(α*(c - maxc)) maybe. Use α = t (e.g., 0.2*n? need scale). c max ~1/n. To make top peaks prominent, α ~ 2n maybe? Since gap between peak and average ~1/n. If α=10n, exp(10) difference. Use w sum=1.
- Compute h = p*L_i where L_i = corr(p,w_shift). Let's derive gradient of F = logsumexp(α c)/α wrt z.
c=f*f. dc/dz = J^T dc/dp. J^T v = p*(v - Σ p v).
dc/dp_i = 2 L_i? Since derivative full convolution c_k: ∂c_k/∂p_i = 2 p_{k-i}? But in c vector, gradient wrt p_i = 2 Σ_k ∂? For soft F, grad_p_i = (1/α) Σ_k w_k ∂c_k/∂p_i = 2 Σ_k w_k p_{k-i} = 2 L_i. (For diagonal terms still factor 2? In sum over k, each pair counted? Let's verify for n=2 p=[a,b], c=[a^2,2ab,b^2]. dF/da for soft =2 a w0 + 2 b w1. Formula 2L_i with L_0 = w0 p0 + w1 p1? corr? For i=0: sum_j p_j w_{0+j}: j=0 p0 w0, j=1 p1 w1 -> L0=a w0+b w1. 2L0 =2a w0+2b w1 correct. For i=1: j=0 p0 w1, j=1 p1 w2 -> b w1+a w2. 2L1=2a w1+2b w2. d c1/db=2a, c2/db=2b; yes. Good.
Then grad_z_i = p_i (grad_p_i - Σ_j p_j grad_p_j) = 2 p_i (L_i - Σ_j p_j L_j). Let meanL = Σ p_j L_j. Update z -= lr * 2 p_i (L_i - meanL). The mean subtraction enforces sum invariance and is Adam on simplex. We can implement vectorized.
- Use Adam with per-coordinate learning rate? Since p_i (L_i-mean) can vary. We can set lr schedule.

Need compute L_i = Σ_j p_j w_{i+j}. As above, L = correlation. Implement fast:
def cross_corr_p_w(p, w):
    # p len n, w len L=2n-1
    # want L_i = sum_{j=0}^{n-1} p_j * w_{i+j}
    # Let w_rev = w[::-1] length L. Conv = np.fft.ifft(fft(p, N)*fft(w_rev, N)).real
    # conv[t] = sum_j p_j w_rev[t-j] = sum_j p_j w[L-1-(t-j)] = sum_j p_j w[L-1-t+j]. Set i = L-1-t => t = L-1-i.
    # So L_i = conv[L-1-i]. Need conv length L? We need t from 0 to L-1.
    # N power of 2 >= L+n-1 = 4n-2? Because convolve p length n and w_rev length L gives length n+L-1=3n-2? Wait p length n, w length 2n-1, convolution length 3n-2. We only need t=0..L-1? L-1=2n-2. That's within.
But maybe easier use FFT direct cross-correlation by zero padding: For L_i = sum_j p_j w_{i+j}, with i=0..n-1. This is like sliding dot product. We can compute by convolving p with w from index? w_rev=[w2,w1,w0]. conv t: t0=a w2; t1=a w1+b w2; t2=a w0+b w1+c? no p len2, w len3, t2 = a w0 + b w1; good. L1=t2? L-1=2. L0? need t? L-1 - i: i=0 -> t2 = L0? gives a w0+b w1 yes. i=1 -> t1 = a w1+b w1? t1 = a w1+b w2 = L1. Good. So L_i = conv[L-1-i] with N enough.

Use FFT for cross-corr.

FFT method for L_i: We can zero-pad p length N and w length N, but w length 2n-1. Need cyclic correlation. If N >= n + (2n-1) -1 = 3n-2, conv length full. We only need n values. N = power2 >= 3n-2. def softmax_from_z(z):
    z = z - np.max(z) (to stabilize). But this changes sum; p=exp(z); p/=p.sum(). For large n, if z has large spread, some underflow to 0; okay but maybe want avoid too small. Could clip z after update.

Adam state: m,z,v state length n. lr schedule. We need update formula:
grad_z = 2 * p * (Lvec - meanL)
meanL = dot(p, Lvec)
But grad_z sums to ~0. We can use Adam with beta1=0.9,beta2=0.999, eps. Step:
m = b1*m + (1-b1)*grad
v = b2*v + (1-b2)*grad^2
mhat=m/(1-b1^t); vhat=v/(1-b2^t)
z -= lr * mhat/(sqrt(vhat)+eps)
z -= z.mean() to keep scale.
Need choose lr. grad magnitude? p ~1/n, L maybe w conv of p. w sum1, p sum1, L_i = E_j[i] p_j? Since w sum1, L is a weighted average of p with shifts? Actually L_i = Σ_j p_j w_{i+j}. Sum_j p_j over j=1, w weights sum1, so L values about average p? meanL maybe ~1/n. Differences maybe small. grad_z =2 p diff ~2*(1/n)*(1/n)=2/n^2. Adam normalizes by rms ~n^-2, step ~lr. Alternative update directly p via normalized exponentiated gradient:
p_new = p * exp(-η * p * (L - meanL) * scale) ; p_new /= sum.
This is like mirror descent with entropy. It may be more stable and n-invariant. The exponent argu = -η * n^2 * p*(L-meanL) maybe O(1). Let's derive. For uniform, p=1/n, L differences O(1/n), product O(1/n^2). Multiply by n^2 to get O(1). So update exponent = -lr * n^2 * p_i (L_i - meanL). Then p_new = p * exp(exponent). Normalize. This is natural step size independent of n. Could be efficient.

We can implement both: option 'entropy' update. It ensures p positive. Use clipping exponent to [-5,5] to avoid blowup. Then normalize. This is similar to mirror descent. Let's use this as primary, perhaps no Adam. It may be faster and stable.

Implementing optimization on coarse kernels length m with entropy/direct should work. We can also include simple parametric anti-symmetric smooth initializations: f_i ∝ 1 + ρ cos(π i/(m)) (or sin) with clipping, for several ρ.

Let's design final program architecture:

Global best: list, score = inf.
Update_best(p): convert to list floats, clip [0,1000], evaluate exact, if finite lower update. Also maybe fast objective for screening.

Functions:
- clip_normalize(p): p=np.asarray float, p=np.clip(p,0,None), s=p.sum(); if s<=1e-12 return None else p/s (sum1)
- fast_score(p): n=len; c_fast=p_self_conv(p); return 2*n*max(c) (since sum1)
- p_self_conv(p): N=next_pow2(2n-1); P=fft(p,N); return ifft(P*P).real[:2n-1]
- correlate_p_w(p,w): N=next_pow2(2n-1); P=fft(p,N); W=fft(w,N); return ifft(P*conj(W)).real[:n]
But we can combine in optimize.

Optimize function for a given p (sum1) and n:
```
def entropy_refine(p, max_time, alpha=10.0, lr_start=1.0, lr_end=0.1, warmup=0.2, log=False):
    p = clip_normalize(p)
    if p is None: return p, inf
    n=p.size
    N=1 << ( (2*n-1).bit_length() -1?) Actually next pow2: 1 << (m.bit_length()-1) if power else ... simpler: N=1; while N<2*n-1: N*=2.
    t0=time.monotonic()
    best_p=p.copy(); best_j=fast_score(p)
    lr=lr_start
    it=0
    stall=0
    while time.monotonic()-t0 < max_time - 1:
        c = fast_conv_from_fft(p,N)
        J = c.max()
        if J < best_j: best_j=J; best_p=p.copy(); stall=0 else stall++
        # weights
        alpha_cur = alpha * n  # since c scale 1/n
        base = alpha_cur*(c-J)
        # To avoid too sparse, floor maybe -50
        w = np.exp(np.clip(base, -50,0))
        s=w.sum()
        if not finite or s==0: w=np.zeros(2n-1); idx=argmax; w[idx]=1
        else w/=s
        # correlate
        L = corr(p,w,N)
        meanL = dot(p,L)
        delta = p*(L-meanL)
        maxd = max(abs(delta))
        if maxd>0:
            # adaptive lr decay:
            frac = min(1,(time.monotonic()-t0)/max_time)
            lr_cur = lr_end + (lr_start-lr_end)*(1-frac)
            exponent = -lr_cur * delta / maxd
            exponent = np.clip(exponent, -3,3)
            p_new = p * np.exp(exponent)
            p_new = np.clip(p_new, 0, None)
            snew=p_new.sum()
            if finite and snew>0: p=p_new/snew
            else: break
        # maybe occasional anti-palindromic/projection? normalize sum
        # every it call evaluate exact maybe expensive if n large? exact conv O(n^2) bad. Use fast score. Only final evaluate external.
        it+=1
    return best_p,best_j
```
But this entropy update with lr scaling by maxd may be too aggressive? Exponent max -lr_cur. lr_start maybe 1.0, clip 3. That can multiply p by e^-1 for worst components, strong. Could oscillate. Use lr_start=0.5, clip 2. We can add momentum? Maybe okay.

However, if n small, maxd scale? We divide by maxd, so step independent. It may not converge to optimum because it always moves along steepest relative component. But with normalization, maybe. We can also do line search: try exponent scale factors [1,0.5,0.25] and accept if fast J decreases. This improves.

Implement in optimize loop:
- Compute proposed p1 for a set of step sizes: lr in [lr_cur, lr_cur*0.5, lr_cur*0.25]
- Evaluate fast J for p1, choose best (least). If all worse, reduce global lr (lr_cur *=0.8) or accept with smaller? We can use "adapt" lr = min(2, lr*1.2) if improvement, else lr*0.7.
This makes robust. Fast score requires conv once per trial (expensive). Instead use one step and maybe backtracking only 2 trials. Trials okay for n small; for n large, one.

We can use fast objective for acceptance; exact rare.

But the entropy update exponent based on delta/maxd can be too local. Alternative: Add a global "flattening" step: after update, p = (1-λ)p + λ*uniform? No, that worsens.

Could use Adam instead of entropy for small n. But entropy is simple.

Let's consider using scipy.optimize.minimize for coarse kernels m<=16 with objective soft-max or max. We can call top-level objective function with global parameters? But no closures. We can write:
```
global_obj_n = None

   p=softmax(z); return softmax_c(p, alpha)
```
Global variables can persist. But rules: helper top-level no closures; global okay. Use scipy if available. But multiple lengths require changing globals; okay.
Scipy minimize with Nelder-Mead or Powell on z length m. Objective non-smooth but okay. However global optimization over 10D maybe slow. We can use differential_evolution for m<=6 maybe.

Maybe a simpler global stochastic search for small m:
For m in [6..20], run many random p (Dirichlet) and block perturbations, using fast score to hill climb. This can find local optima. Since m small, each eval O(m^2) trivial; 2400s can do millions. But random global may not find target; hill climbing from many starts can.

We can combine: For small m, use a random search with acceptance (simulated annealing) over p normalized. Operations:
- Random Dirichlet initialization
- Local move: add noise to a few coordinates, project to simplex (clip, normalize), or logit Gaussian.
- If score improves accept; else accept with annealing probability.
This is like MCMC. For small m, can explore global. We can run several m.

But to guarantee improvement, we need use structured initializations too. The previous block perturbation from uniform got stuck at 2.0; structured half-block gives 1.777. We need below1.5; likely need full random/local enough.

Let's think of using CMA-ES? Not available unless scipy.optimize. SciPy has minimize with method='Powell' or 'Nelder-Mead' which can find local minima for small dimensions. We can use many restarts. Objective: E(p) = 2n*max(conv(p,p)) with p sum1,p>=0. Use variables y length m-1 (last=1-sum). Bounds? Use soft transform x -> exp(x) normalized to ensure p>0,sum1. This avoids bounds. Objective with soft max smooth for optimization:
F(z) = soft_max(α c) maybe. Use scipy minimize BFGS? Need gradient; can use finite diff. For m up to 20, Nelder-Mead finite diff okay. But many restarts.

Maybe implement our own coordinate descent? For small m, we can minimize max by active set linearization:
At current p, find active set A (top conv positions). Approximate each c_k as linear in p? c_k quadratic; but we can solve a convex quadratic? Not.

Let's evaluate possibility of using cvxpy for small m: SDP? Objective max quadratic not convex. No.

Another mathematical idea: Since all functions nonnegative, the optimal p might be a "minimax" solution of a linear programming over squared variables. We can linearize by setting x_i = p_i^2? Not exact because off-diagonal products.

What about using matrix scaling to solve L_i=μ? We can iterate:
Given p, active weights w. Solve for p_new such that L_i(p_new)=μ and sum=1? That's a fixed point of a nonlinear? L_i = Σ_j p_new_j w_{i+j}. This is linear in p_new! For given w and μ, solving L=μ, sum=1, p≥0 is a linear feasibility. We can choose μ as large as possible? Actually we want p_new to reduce peaks; maybe set L_i constant on support. We can solve linear program: maximize μ subject to L_i ≤ μ? But L is linear in p. However constant L over support corresponds making gradient zero for weighted active peaks. If we choose w = normalized active peaks, and require L_i=μ for all i with p_i>0, this is a system of n equations. We can solve by EC? For full support, n equations plus sum constraint overdetermined; but w length 2n-1. Maybe choose w as a filter whose convolution with p is flat. If we can find w such that p = solve, and w derived from p*c? fixed.

Alternative: Use "flat top filter" construction: choose w (active weights) first, solve p via linear deconvolution, then compute c and update w. Let w be a probability over lags. Solve P(z) * W_rev(z) = μ (1 + z + ... + z^{n-1}) (since L_i=μ). In z-transform, P(z) = μ (1-z^n)/(1-z) / W_rev(z). Need p nonnegative and sum1. If w is chosen such that this ratio polynomial nonnegative. This is like designing p and w as dual. We can choose w to be a window (e.g., top active set) and compute p. Iterate.

Maybe a simple closed form: If w is uniform over all lags (1/(2n-1)), W_rev(z) = (1/(2n-1))(1 - z^{2n-1})/(1-z). Then P(z) = μ (1-z^n)/(1-z) * (2n-1)/(1-z^{2n-1}). This is not polynomial finite? It is rational, p extends infinite. Not good.

If w is a delta at lag n-1 (center active), W_rev(z)=z^{?}. P(z)= μ (1-z^n)/(1-z) z^? => uniform p, local optimum. If w is delta at edge lag 0, P polynomial? W_rev=z^{2n-2}, P=μ(1-z^n)/(1-z) z^{-(2n-2)} not causal. Need w supported such that division yields polynomial length n. This is factorization problem.

Maybe the optimal p corresponds to a "Zerofree complement" where active set w is a delta pair? If active peaks at two symmetric lags, w = α δ_k + (1-α)δ_l. Then W_rev(z)=α z^? + (1-α)z^?. P(z)=μ (1-z^n)/(1-z) / W. For P polynomial of degree n-1, W must divide numerator? Not generally. If w is a geometric progression, maybe.

This is getting theoretical.

Let's return to practical: We need code likely to find good. We can incorporate a brute-force/optimization for small m using many iterations and hope. Since we cannot run, need make algorithm as powerful as possible within constraints.

Use global variables and multiple phases:
1. Logic to generate candidate sequences using:
   - Uniform (score2)
   - Half-block with ratio r optimized? Use analytic [r...r,1...1] for even m; can quick evaluate via function half_block(m, r). r can range [0.1,5]. We can sweep r for m small. Also reversed block.
   - 3-block [a,b,c] with lengths ratios and value ratios, maybe coarse grid.
   - Smooth anti-symmetric kernels: cosine/sine: p_i ∝ 1 + ρ cos(π i/(m)) clipped, for ρ in [0,0.2,...,1], and variants.
   - Random Dirichlet seeds.
2. For each candidate, run a fast local optimizer (entropy or stochastic) for a time slice. Keep best.
3. For best coarse length, try block upscaling to larger lengths and evaluate exact. Also maybe optimize the upscaled sequence with FFT entropy for remaining time to refine.

But time allocation? We can use runtime dynamic.

Let's design a robust local optimizer for both small and medium. For small m, use direct conv and stochastic local search (block/coordinate) with many evaluations; for medium, use FFT entropy.

Small direct optimizer possibilities:
- Since m <= 32, direct conv via np.convolve O(m^2) ~1k, can do 100k eps/sec. We can do many restarts and anneal.
- Simulated annealing on logit vector:
   z = log(p) (length m). p=softmax(z). Objective E. Move: z += lr * normal(0,1) (or random sparse). p normalized automatically. Accept if E_new < E_old or with annealing prob. We can also use line search. This explores global. For m small, good.
- Use multiple cooling schedules and random restarts. We can allocate e.g. 600s to small m search (m=8..24) to find target. But if target not found, still use entropy on medium.

Let's improve entropy optimizer: Use log-sum-exp objective with adaptive temperature and line search. We can implement on p directly with direct/FFT.

Pseudo for `entropy_refine(p, time_limit, use_fft=True)`:
```
p = clip_normalize(p)
n=p.size
N=next_pow2(2*n-1) if n>=64 else None
best=p.copy(); best_score=score(p)
lr=0.5
alpha=10.0 # times n
cool=0
while time<limit:
   c = conv(p)
   J=c.max()
   # accept if best
   base = alpha*n*(c-J)
   w = exp(clip(base,-40,0)); w/=w.sum()
   L = corr(p,w)
   grad = p*(L - dot(p,L))
   maxg = max(abs(grad))
   if maxg>0:
      for scale in [lr, lr*0.5, lr*0.25]:
         p_new = p * exp(clip(-scale*grad/maxg,-4,4))
         score=score(p_new)
         if score < best_score or (score < current and accept): best...
      if accepted: p=best_step
      else: lr*=0.7
   else break
   # anneal perturb if no improvement for many: p = softmax(log(p + eps) + small noise)
```
But score calls each trial expensive. We can do one trial and adjust lr based on J not full? J=soft? For acceptance need J. We can compute score(p_new) with conv. 3 trials per iter triples cost. For n medium, maybe one trial; for small, 3. Use `tries` arg.

What about gradient sign? We use exponent -scale*grad. If grad positive (above mean L), decrease p_i. Good.

Potential oscillation. Add momentum to old_unnormalized logp:
```
logp = np.log(p+eps)
v = 0.9*v - 0.1*grad/maxg
logp += v? 
p=exp(logp); normalize
```
Maybe too much.

Alternatively, use Nesterov? Keep simple.

   - d = rng.normal(size=m); d -= d.mean(); p_new = p + step*d; p_new = np.clip(p_new,0,None); s=p_new.sum(); if s>0 normalize.
This is a random walk on simplex. Adaptive step.
Also coordinate moves: pick random i,j, move mass ε from i to j. Global random noise better for exploration.

def p_corr_w_direct(p,w):
 n=len(p); L=np.empty(n)
 for i in range(n):
    L[i]=np.dot(p, w[i:i+n])
 return L
```

Candidate types for a given n:
1. uniform: ones/n.
2. half_block(n, ratio): m1=n//2, m2=n-m1. Fill first m1=ratio, second=1. Normalize. Use ratio values [1.0,1.5,2.0,2.5,3.0]. Also reversed (first low, second high).
3. cosine_anti(n, rho): base=1 + rho*cos(pi*i/(n-1))? To get high first or low. Use both signs: rho values [0.1..1]. Ensure nonnegative: 1+rho*cos has min 1-|rho|, so |rho|<=1. Normalize. For n=1 handle.
4. sine maybe: base=1 + rho*sin(pi*i/(n-1))? Sin is palindrome? Could help? Use.
5. Cubic/smooth: base=1 + rho*(2*x-1) where x=i/(n-1) (linear anti), rho [0.2,0.5,0.8,1]. Nonneg if rho<=1; but linear ramp min 0.
6. 3-block: lengths roughly thirds, values pattern [a,b,c], with a>c. Try ratios: first high=2, mid=1.5/1, low=1? Normalize. Multiple.
7. Random Dirichlet with shape 1, or anti-symmetric random: generate a anti-palindromic normalized, f=1+rho*a clipped.

Let's refine gradient refine for small/medium.

Pseudo code:
```
def candidate_score_direct(p):
    p = normalize_nonnegative(p)
    if p is None: return inf, p
    c = np.convolve(p,p)
    return float(2*p.size*c.max()), p

def fft_conv_p(p,N):
    return np.fft.ifft(np.fft.fft(p,N)*np.fft.fft(p,N)).real[:2*p.size-1]

def fft_corr_p_w(p,w,N):
    P=np.fft.fft(p,N); W=np.fft.fft(w,N); return np.fft.ifft(P*np.conj(W)).real[:p.size]

def gradient_refine(p, time_limit, seed=0, verbose=False):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    p=normalize(p); if None return
    n=p.size
    use_fft = n >= 96
    if use_fft: N=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    current=fast_score(p)
    best=p.copy(); best_score=current
    scale=0.5
    no_imp=0
    alpha_factor=10.0
    last_eval=time
    iter=0
    while time < limit:
       c = conv(p)
       J = c.max()
       if J*2*n? current=2*n*J vs best
       if current < best_score: best... no_imp=0 else no_imp++
       if no_imp>50: # perturb
           p = normalize((1-0.1)*p + 0.1*rng.random(n))
           scale=0.5
           no_imp=0
           continue
       base = alpha_factor*n*(c-J)
       w = exp(clip(base,-50,0)); wsum=w.sum(); if wsum>0 w/=wsum else w[argmax]=1
       L = corr(p,w)
       meanL = np.dot(p,L)
       delta = p*(L-meanL)
       maxd = np.max(np.abs(delta))
       if maxd < 1e-300: break
       # try scales
       best_trial = None; best_trial_score = current
       for s in (scale, 0.5*scale, 0.25*scale):
           expo = np.clip(-s*delta/maxd, -3,3)
           p_new = p*np.exp(expo)
           snew = p_new.sum()
           if snew<=0: continue
           p_new = p_new/snew
           sc = score(p_new)
           if sc < best_trial_score:
               best_trial_score=sc; best_trial=p_new
       if best_trial is not None:
           # accept if improved over current, or occasionally not too worse
           if best_trial_score <= current or (best_trial_score < best_score and rng.random()<0.1):
               p=best_trial; current=best_trial_score
               scale=min(3.0, scale*1.1)
           else:
               scale=max(0.05, scale*0.7)
       iter+=1
       # periodic exact? no
    return best,best_score
```
Need score(p_new) computes conv each trial. For large n, 3 trials expensive. We can set tries=1 for n>512, tries=3 for n<128. Adaptive.

If weights too sparse, delta sparse but okay. Maybe start 8 and slowly increase: alpha_factor = min(20, 4 + 16*progress). But varying may hurt. Use 10.

Step scale: Using delta/maxd normalizes; scale 0.5 means worst component multiplied by exp(-0.5). Reasonable.

Could `delta` have large maxd due to a few p small? It is product p*L; small p small. Fine.

Let's define helper functions carefully:
```
def norm_nonnegative(v):
    x=np.asarray(v,dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size==0: return None
    x=np.where(x>0,x,0.0)
    s=x.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=0: return None
    x=x/s
    return x
```
Clipping negative before sum. Values could be huge? Normalize.

`next_pow2(m)`:
```
def next_pow2(m):
    n=1
    while n<m: n*=2
    return n
```

`convolve_p(p, N=None)`:
```
def convolve_p(p,N=None):
   n=p.size
   if n < 128:
      return np.convolve(p,p)
   if N is None: N=next_pow2(2*n-1)
   P=np.fft.fft(p,N)
   c=np.fft.ifft(P*P).real
   return c[:2*n-1]
```

def make_inits(n, rng, count=30):
    inits=[]
    # uniform
    inits.append(norm_nonnegative(np.ones(n)))
    # half
    for r in [0.5,0.8,1.25,1.75,2.5,3.5]:
       m=n//2; p=np.ones(n); p[:m]=r; inits.append(norm)
       p=np.ones(n); p[m:]=r; inits.append(norm)
    # cosine sign both
    x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    for rho in [0.1,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9,1.0]:
       p=1+rho*np.cos(np.pi*x); inits.append(norm)
       p=1-rho*np.cos(np.pi*x); inits.append(norm)
    # linear
    for rho in [0.2,0.5,0.8,1.0]:
       p=1+rho*(1-2*x); inits
       p=1-rho*(1-2*x)
    # quadratic anti: p=1+rho*(1-2x)-? maybe
    # random
    for _ in range(4): inits.append(norm(rng.random(n)))
    for _ in range(2): p=rng.dirichlet? use rng.random normalized
    # three block
    m1=n//3; m2=2*n//3; m3=n-m2
    for a,b,c in [(3,2,1),(2.5,1.5,1),(4,2,1),(2,1,0.5),(3,1.5,1)]:
       p[:m1]=a; p[m1:m2]=b; p[m2:]=c; inits
    # anti random
    a=rng.normal(size=n); a = a - a[::-1]; if maxabs>0 p = 1 + 0.7*a/maxabs; clip; inits
```

Implementation of gradient_refine in detail:
```
def gradient_refine(p, time_limit, seed=0, verbose=False):
    p = norm_nonnegative(p)
    if p is None: return p, np.inf
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    n = p.size
    use_fft = n >= 128
    N = next_pow2(2*n-1) if use_fft else None
    Lc = 2*n-1
    if use_fft:
       current = score_p(p, True, N)
    else:
       current = score_p(p, False, None)
    best_p = p.copy(); best_score=current
    scale=0.5
    no_imp=0
    max_no_imp=100 if n<256 else 40
    t0=time.monotonic()
    local_deadline=t0+time_limit
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < local_deadline:
       # compute c
       if use_fft:
          P=np.fft.fft(p,N)
          c=np.fft.ifft(P*P).real[:Lc]
       else:
          c=np.convolve(p,p)
       J=float(np.max(c))
       current=2.0*n*J
       if current < best_score - 1e-12:
           best_score=current; best_p=p.copy(); no_imp=0
       else:
           no_imp +=1
       if no_imp > max_no_imp:
           eps = 0.1 if n<256 else 0.03
           p = norm_nonnegative((1.0-eps)*p + eps*rng.random(n))
           if p is None: break
           scale=0.5
           no_imp=0
           continue
       # weights
       base = 10.0*n*(c-J)
       w1 = np.exp(np.clip(base, -50.0, 0.0))
       s1 = np.sum(w1)
       if s1 <= 0 or not np.isfinite(s1): w1 = np.zeros(Lc); w1[0]=1? But max at argmax.
       else: w1 /= s1
       # top mix
       k = max(1, int(0.05*Lc) + 1)
       if k >= Lc:
           w2 = np.ones(Lc)/Lc
       else:
           # np.argpartition(c, -k) but if k small
           idx = np.argpartition(c, Lc-k)[Lc-k:]
           w2 = np.zeros(Lc); w2[idx]=1.0; w2/=k
       w = 0.5*w1 + 0.5*w2
       ws = w.sum()
       if ws>0: w/=ws
       else: w=np.zeros(Lc); w[0]=1
       # corr
       if use_fft:
          P=np.fft.fft(p,N) # recompute P? We have P from before; can reuse! But if we computed P before c, use same. Let's store P0 and use for W? P unchanged.
          W=np.fft.fft(w,N)
          Lvec=np.fft.ifft(P0 * np.conj(W)).real[:n]
       else:
          Lvec=corr_p_w_direct(p,w)
       meanL=float(np.dot(p,Lvec))
       delta = p*(Lvec-meanL)
       maxd = float(np.max(np.abs(delta)))
       if maxd < 1e-300:
          break
       # trials
       trial_best=None; trial_score=current
       if n < 256:
          scales=(scale, scale*0.5, scale*0.25)
       elif n < 1024:
          scales=(scale, scale*0.5)
       else:
          scales=(scale,)
       for sc in scales:
          expo=np.clip(-sc*delta/maxd, -3.0,3.0)
          p_new = p*np.exp(expo)
          p_new = np.clip(p_new, 0.0, None)
          snew=p_new.sum()
          if snew <= 0 or not np.isfinite(snew): continue
          p_new = p_new/snew
          if use_fft:
             scnew=score_p(p_new, True, N)
          else:
             scnew=score_p(p_new, False, None)
          if scnew < trial_score:
             trial_score=scnew; trial_best=p_new
       if trial_best is not None:
          # Accept if improved over current, or if it improves global a lot with prob?
          if trial_score <= current or (trial_score < best_score*0.999 and rng.random() < 0.1):
             p=trial_best; current=trial_score
             scale = min(2.5, scale*1.05)
          else:
             scale = max(0.05, scale*0.8)
       it+=1
       if verbose and it % 100 == 0: print(...)
    return best_p, best_score
```

def score_normalized(p, use_fft=False,N=None):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.maximum(p,0.0)
    n=p.size
    if use_fft:
       if N is None: N=next_pow2(2*n-1)
       P=np.fft.fft(p,N)
       c=np.fft.ifft(P*P).real[:2*n-1]
    else:
       c=np.convolve(p,p)
    m = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(m): return float('inf')

    if not cands: return []
    arr=[]
    for i,(s,p) in enumerate(cands):
       arr.append((s,i,p))
    arr.sort()
    res=[]
    for s,i,p in arr:
       res.append((s,p))
    return res

```python
"""
Algorithm summary:
- Normalize candidates to sum 1; the score is scale invariant.
- Search over several sequence lengths (small to a few thousand).
- Build structured non-palindromic initials: half-block, anti-tonic cosine/linear,
  off-center Gaussian bumps, three-level blocks, random simplex starts, etc.
- Refine with an entropy/multiplicative local search:
    * compute c = p*p
    * build a hybrid active-set weight w from top entries + softmax of c
    * compute L_i = sum_j p_j w_{i+j}
    * move p_i multiplicatively against p_i(L_i - mean p L)
- Keep global best candidates and exact-verify the best few at the end.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def norm_nonnegative(x):
    """Return a normalized nonnegative float vector with sum 1, or None."""
    a = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1 or a.size == 0:
        return None
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return None
    a /= s
    return a


def next_pow2(m):
    n = 1
    while n < m:
        n <<= 1
    return n


def score_normalized(p, use_fft=False, N=None):
    """
    Score for an already normalized p (sum = 1):
        E = 2*n*max(p*p).
    """
    a = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    n = a.size
    if use_fft:
        if N is None or N < 2 * n - 1:
            N = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
        P = np.fft.fft(a, N)
        c = np.fft.ifft(P * P).real[:2 * n - 1]
    else:
        c = np.convolve(a, a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * m)


def score_p(p, use_fft=False, N=None):
    """Normalize p and score it."""
    q = norm_nonnegative(p)
    if q is None:
        return float("inf")
    return score_normalized(q, use_fft, N)


def corr_p_w_direct(p, w):
    """
    L_i = sum_j p_j w_{i+j}, i=0..n-1.
    Direct O(n^2), used for small n.
    """
    n = p.size
    out = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(n):
        out[i] = float(np.dot(p, w[i:i + n]))
    return out


def corr_p_w_fft(p, w, N):
    """
    L_i = sum_j p_j w_{i+j}, i=0..n-1, via FFT.
    Requires N >= 2*n-1.
    """
    P = np.fft.fft(p, N)
    W = np.fft.fft(w, N)
    return np.fft.ifft(P * np.conj(W)).real[:p.size]


def gradient_refine(p, time_limit, seed=0, verbose=False):
    """
    Multiplicative/entropy local refinement of a normalized sequence.
    Returns (best_p, best_score).
    """
    p = norm_nonnegative(p)
    if p is None:
        return p, float("inf")

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    n = p.size
    use_fft = n >= 128
    N = next_pow2(2 * n - 1) if use_fft else None
    Lc = 2 * n - 1

    current = score_normalized(p, use_fft, N)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = current

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(1e-6, float(time_limit))
    scale = 0.5
    no_imp = 0
    iters = 0

    max_no_imp = 90 if n < 256 else 35

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if use_fft:
            P0 = np.fft.fft(p, N)
            c = np.fft.ifft(P0 * P0).real[:Lc]
        else:
            c = np.convolve(p, p)

        J = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
        current = float(2.0 * n * J)

        if current < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = current
            best_p = p.copy()
            no_imp = 0
        else:
            no_imp += 1

        if no_imp > max_no_imp:
            eps = 0.12 if n < 256 else 0.035
            base = best_p
            p = norm_nonnegative((1.0 - eps) * base + eps * rng.random(n))
            if p is None:
                break
            current = score_normalized(p, use_fft, N)
            scale = 0.5
            no_imp = 0
            if verbose:
                print("  perturbed, current=%.6f best=%.6f" % (current, best_score))
            continue

        # Hybrid weights: softmax on peaks plus top-k active lags.
        base_w = 10.0 * n * (c - J)
        w1 = np.exp(np.clip(base_w, -50.0, 0.0))
        s1 = float(np.sum(w1))
        if s1 > 0.0 and np.isfinite(s1):
            w1 /= s1
        else:
            w1 = np.zeros(Lc, dtype=np.float64)
            w1[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0

        k = max(1, int(0.05 * Lc) + 1)
        if k >= Lc:
            w2 = np.ones(Lc, dtype=np.float64) / float(Lc)
        else:
            idx = np.argpartition(c, Lc - k)[Lc - k:]
            w2 = np.zeros(Lc, dtype=np.float64)
            w2[idx] = 1.0
            w2 /= float(k)

        w = 0.5 * w1 + 0.5 * w2
        ws = float(np.sum(w))
        if ws > 0.0 and np.isfinite(ws):
            w /= ws
        else:
            w = np.zeros(Lc, dtype=np.float64)
            w[0] = 1.0

        if use_fft:
            Lvec = corr_p_w_fft(p, w, N)
        else:
            Lvec = corr_p_w_direct(p, w)

        meanL = float(np.dot(p, Lvec))
        delta = p * (Lvec - meanL)
        maxd = float(np.max(np.abs(delta))) if delta.size else 0.0
        if maxd < 1e-300:
            break

        if n < 256:
            trial_scales = (scale, 0.5 * scale, 0.25 * scale)
        elif n < 1024:
            trial_scales = (scale, 0.5 * scale)
        else:
            trial_scales = (scale,)

        best_trial = None
        best_trial_score = current

        for ts in trial_scales:
            expo = np.clip(-ts * delta / maxd, -3.0, 3.0)
            p_new = p * np.exp(expo)
            p_new = np.maximum(p_new, 0.0)
            snew = float(p_new.sum())
            if not np.isfinite(snew) or snew <= 0.0:
                continue
            p_new /= snew
            sc_new = score_normalized(p_new, use_fft, N)
            if sc_new < best_trial_score:
                best_trial_score = sc_new
                best_trial = p_new

        if best_trial is not None:
            if best_trial_score <= current or (best_trial_score < best_score and rng.random() < 0.12):
                p = best_trial
                current = best_trial_score
                scale = min(2.5, scale * 1.08)
            else:
                scale = max(0.04, scale * 0.8)

        iters += 1
        if verbose and (iters % 50 == 0):
            print("    n=%d iter=%d current=%.6f best=%.6f" % (n, iters, current, best_score))

    return best_p, best_score


def make_inits(n, rng, rich=True):
    """
    Return a list of normalized initial sequences of length n.
    """
    inits = []
    if n <= 0:
        return inits

    # Uniform.
    inits.append(norm_nonnegative(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)))

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64) if n > 1 else np.array([0.0], dtype=np.float64)

    # Half-block patterns, forward and reverse.
    ratios = [0.5, 0.8, 1.25, 1.75, 2.25, 3.0, 4.0]
    if not rich:
        ratios = [1.0, 2.0, 3.0]
    m = n // 2
    for r in ratios:
        p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        p[:m] = r
        inits.append(norm_nonnegative(p))

        if n - m > 0:
            q = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
            q[m:] = r
            inits.append(norm_nonnegative(q))

    # Anti-palindromic smooth ramps.
    rhos = [0.1, 0.25, 0.4, 0.55, 0.7, 0.85, 1.0]
    if not rich:
        rhos = [0.2, 0.5, 0.8, 1.0]
    if n > 1:
        for rho in rhos:
            p = 1.0 + rho * np.cos(np.pi * x)
            inits.append(norm_nonnegative(p))
            p = 1.0 - rho * np.cos(np.pi * x)
            inits.append(norm_nonnegative(p))

            p = 1.0 + rho * (1.0 - 2.0 * x)
            inits.append(norm_nonnegative(p))
            p = 1.0 - rho * (1.0 - 2.0 * x)
            inits.append(norm_nonnegative(p))

    # Three-block patterns.
    m1 = n // 3
    m2 = 2 * n // 3
    if m1 > 0 and (n - m2) > 0:
        blocks = [
            (3.0, 2.0, 1.0),
            (2.5, 1.5, 1.0),
            (4.0, 2.0, 1.0),
            (2.0, 1.5, 0.75),
            (3.5, 1.5, 1.0)
        ]
        if not rich:
            blocks = blocks[:2]
        for a, b, c in blocks:
            p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
            p[:m1] = a
            p[m1:m2] = b
            p[m2:] = c
            inits.append(norm_nonnegative(p))

            q = p.copy()
            q[:m1] = c
            q[m1:m2] = b
            q[m2:] = a
            inits.append(norm_nonnegative(q))

    # Off-center Gaussian bumps with optional skew.
    if rich:
        centers = [0.22, 0.35, 0.55, 0.78]
        widths = [0.10, 0.18, 0.30, 0.45]
        skews = [-0.7, 0.0, 0.7]
        for cf in centers:
            for wd in widths:
                for sk in skews:
                    if n > 1:
                        base = np.exp(-0.5 * ((x - cf) / wd) ** 2)
                        p = base * (1.0 + sk * (x - cf))
                        inits.append(norm_nonnegative(p))
                    else:
                        inits.append(norm_nonnegative(np.array([1.0])))

    # Random simplex starts.
    nrand = 6 if rich else 3
    for _ in range(nrand):
        p = rng.random(n, dtype=np.float64)
        inits.append(norm_nonnegative(p))

    # Random anti-palindromic shape.
    for _ in range(2 if rich else 1):
        a = rng.normal(size=n, dtype=(np.float64 if n > 1 else np.float64))
        if n > 1:
            a = a - a[::-1]
            mx = float(np.max(np.abs(a)))
            if mx > 0.0:
                a = 0.8 * a / mx
                p = 1.0 + a
                inits.append(norm_nonnegative(p))
            else:
                inits.append(norm_nonnegative(np.ones(n)))
        else:
            inits.append(norm_nonnegative(np.array([1.0])))

    out = []
    for v in inits:
        if v is not None:
            out.append(v)
    return out


def add_candidate(score, p, top_candidates, max_keep=100):
    """Add (score,p) to top_candidates and prune the worst."""
    if p is None or not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size == 0:
        return
    top_candidates.append((float(score), p.copy()))
    if len(top_candidates) > max_keep:
        worst = 0
        worst_s = top_candidates[0][0]
        for i in range(len(top_candidates)):
            s = top_candidates[i][0]
            if s > worst_s:
                worst = i
                worst_s = s
        del top_candidates[worst]


def get_best_fast(top_candidates):
    if not top_candidates:
        return None, float("inf")
    best_i = 0
    best_s = top_candidates[0][0]
    for i in range(len(top_candidates)):
        s = top_candidates[i][0]
        if s < best_s:
            best_i = i
            best_s = s
    return top_candidates[best_i][1], best_s


def sort_candidates_by_score(top_candidates):
    if not top_candidates:
        return []
    arr = []
    for i, (s, p) in enumerate(top_candidates):
        arr.append((s, i, p))
    arr.sort()
    return [(s, p) for s, i, p in arr]


def search_length(n, time_limit, seed, top_candidates):
    """Generate starts for length n, refine the best few, update top_candidates."""
    if n <= 0 or time_limit < 2.0:
        return
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    rich = time_limit > 30.0

    inits = make_inits(n, rng, rich=rich)
    use_fft = n >= 128
    N = next_pow2(2 * n - 1) if use_fft else None

    scored = []
    for i, p in enumerate(inits):
        sc = score_p(p, use_fft, N)
        scored.append((sc, i))

    scored.sort()

    # Number of refinements to run from this length.
    if time_limit < 20:
        k = 1
    elif time_limit < 80:
        k = 2
    else:
        k = 3
    k = min(k, len(scored))

    alloc_each = max(3.0, (time_limit - 2.0) / max(1, k))
    t_start = time.monotonic()

    for pos in range(k):
        sc0, idx0 = scored[pos]
        p0 = inits[idx0]
        rem = time_limit - (time.monotonic() - t_start)
        if rem < 2.0:
            break
        # Slightly favor the first (best) initial.
        frac = alloc_each
        if pos == 0:
            frac = max(3.0, 1.3 * alloc_each)
        p_best, sc_best = gradient_refine(p0, min(frac, rem), seed=seed * 1000 + 17 * n + 31 * pos)
        if np.isfinite(sc_best):
            add_candidate(sc_best, p_best, top_candidates)
            # Also keep the initial if it was better.
            if np.isfinite(sc0) and sc0 < sc_best:
                add_candidate(sc0, p0, top_candidates)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Main search entry point.
    Develops low-score sequences by combining structured initials,
    multiplicative local search, and final exact verification.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t_global = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t_global + max(60.0, float(budget_s) - 25.0)

    top_candidates = []

    # Baseline: a sequence that is known to be valid and already beats uniform.
    # For any even n, [2,...,2,1,...,1] gives E = 16/9.
    baseline_n = 96
    mhalf = baseline_n // 2
    base = np.ones(baseline_n, dtype=np.float64)
    base[:mhalf] = 2.0
    base = norm_nonnegative(base)
    if base is not None:
        add_candidate(score_p(base, False, None), base, top_candidates)

    # Use provided previous construction if available and reasonably sized.
    try:
        hp = height_sequence_1
    except Exception:
        hp = None

    if hp is not None:
        try:
            hpv = np.asarray(hp, dtype=np.float64)
            if hpv.ndim == 1 and hpv.size > 0 and hpv.size <= 4096:
                hpv = norm_nonnegative(hpv)
                if hpv is not None:
                    sc = score_p(hpv, hpv.size >= 128)
                    add_candidate(sc, hpv, top_candidates)
        except Exception:
            pass

    # If the schedule gets too close to the deadline, leave room for finalization.
    # Selected lengths and desired time slices. Small lengths are cheap and can
    # reveal strong finite-size effects; medium/large lengths are optimized with FFT.
    schedule = [
        (8,   25),
        (12,  25),
        (16,  35),
        (20,  40),
        (24,  45),
        (32,  70),
        (48,  60),
        (64,  80),
        (96,  90),
        (128, 100),
        (192, 110),
        (256, 140),
        (384, 160),
        (512, 200),
        (768, 250),
        (1024, 300),
        (1536, 350),
        (2048, 450),
        (3072, 550),
        (4096, 650),
    ]

    # Do not spend all time if we already have an excellent candidate; still polish.
    for n, desired in schedule:
        now = time.monotonic()
        remaining = deadline - now
        if remaining < 20.0:
            break
        t = min(float(desired), remaining * 0.95)
        # If current best is already under the requested upper bound, favor polishing.
        best_seq, best_fast = get_best_fast(top_candidates)
        if best_seq is not None and best_fast < 1.5027:
            # Spend less on far-away lengths, more on moderate sizes for polishing.
            if n > 1024:
                t = min(t, 60.0)
            elif n <= 256:
                t = min(t, 90.0)
        try:
            search_length(n, t, int(seed + 1000 * n), top_candidates)
        except Exception:
            # Keep going if one length fails for any reason.
            pass

    # Final polishing pass on the current best candidates.
    now = time.monotonic()
    remaining = deadline - now
    if remaining > 60.0:
        sorted_tops = sort_candidates_by_score(top_candidates)
        # Polish up to two distinct best sequences.
        polished = 0
        for sc, p in sorted_tops:
            if polished >= 2:
                break
            rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
            if rem < 60.0:
                break
            p_seg = norm_nonnegative(p)
            if p_seg is None:
                continue
            # Cap polishing length to keep runtime predictable.
            if p_seg.size > 2048:
                # Use a downsampled version by averaging blocks, then refine it.
                target = 1024
                k = max(1, p_seg.size // target)
                flat = p_seg.reshape(-1, k)
                if flat.size == p_seg.size:
                    down = np.mean(flat, axis=1)
                else:
                    down = p_seg[::k]
                down = norm_nonnegative(down)
                if down is not None:
                    p_seg = down
            t_polish = min(rem * 0.9, 900.0 if p_seg.size <= 512 else 450.0)
            final_p, final_sc = gradient_refine(p_seg, t_polish, seed=int(seed * 7919 + p_seg.size))
            if np.isfinite(final_sc):
                add_candidate(final_sc, final_p, top_candidates)
            polished += 1

    # Choose exact evaluated best. There is no external constraint on length,
    # but the external evaluator uses direct convolution, so keep final length moderate.
    best_exact_seq = None
    best_exact_score = float("inf")

    sorted_tops = sort_candidates_by_score(top_candidates)
    checked = 0
    for sc, p in sorted_tops:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if deadline - now < 10.0:
            break
        if checked >= 12:
            break
        p = norm_nonnegative(p)
        if p is None:
            continue
        if p.size > 4096:
            continue
        try:
            exact = float(evaluate_sequence(p.tolist()))
        except Exception:
            exact = float("inf")
        checked += 1
        if np.isfinite(exact) and exact < best_exact_score:
            best_exact_score = exact
            best_exact_seq = p

    # Guarantee a valid fallback if exact checking did not yield anything usable.
    if best_exact_seq is None:
        best_seq, _ = get_best_fast(top_candidates)
        if best_seq is not None:
            best_seq = norm_nonnegative(best_seq)
        if best_seq is None:
            # Last-resort valid sequence.
            p = np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64)
            p[:32] = 2.0
            best_seq = norm_nonnegative(p)

    # Scale to a comfortable magnitude. Score is scale invariant; this avoids
    # any possible under/overflow in the external evaluator.
    out = np.asarray(best_exact_seq if best_exact_seq is not None else best_seq, dtype=np.float64)
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    s = float(out.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        out = np.ones(64, dtype=np.float64)
        out[:32] = 2.0
        s = out.sum()
    # Put max around 1.0 (well below 1000).
    out = out / s
    out = np.minimum(out, 1000.0)

    return [float(v) for v in out]
```
